E0028

A studio making a game

Loopforge already had approved art, models, lore and an opening in progress when this studio began its retained record. The studio produced candidates, builds, writing, images and reviews within that continuing project. The owner later dropped the unfinished opening goal and made this dev blog the sole objective. The historical production rules below explain how the earlier work was organized; they do not authorize it to restart.

Paragraph referencesE0024, E0025, E0117, E0187, E0370, E0414

This extended record closes on 22 October 2026 at 22:09:57 UTC. It preserves the first history through 21:20:13, adds the goal replacement and all nine craft interviews, and retains every earlier event in a remapped timeline. Delivered assets, passing checks and accepted scenes remain distinct.

Paragraph referencesE0028, E0177, E0219, E0360, E0368, E0370, E0414

Open the historical review — before the later private fixes
Reported ready on 22 October; current availability not rechecked

Who makes the decisions

Producer assigns a bounded piece of work: its owner, exact inputs, permitted changes, expected artifact and review. Specialists work on isolated candidates. Engine Master prepares integration and build instructions; Writer handles voice and continuity; Animator handles movement and contact. Scene Director reviews framing and sequence, while Critic gives a separate judgment of the supplied work. Visual Artist, 3D Artist, Systems Designer and Level Designer cover their own parts of the game. Being hired does not mean a role has begun production.

Paragraph referencesE0062, E0094, E0104, E0116, E0125, E0128, E0129

There are two working contexts. Native Hive agents belong to this studio. A separate, owner-facing Codex chat supplied early integration and later desktop execution, browser captures and host operations. Its reports are identified as bridge evidence. It is not another native specialist, and its unlogged token usage is excluded from the native accounting.

Paragraph referencesE0024, E0026, E0091, E0135, E0177

When the studio took production ownership, a read-only canonical checkout still prevented Engine Master from writing there. The response was a private candidate preserving the existing pending files, followed by an explicit execution handoff. Ownership did not grant permission to discard earlier work or bypass the boundary.

What counts as evidence

A handoff names the version reviewed and the limits of the result. Source checks can prove that lines match, files are intact or a motion is present in a delivery. Numerical checks can find a collision. Neither establishes whether a gesture reads naturally, a line reaches its listener or the player can understand a scene. Those questions need the actual scene and an appropriate review.

Paragraph referencesE0047, E0083, E0097, E0214, E0236

Critic receives the creative artifact before the author’s dedicated explanation. That order helped keep the opening review narrow: two dialogue changes were judged ready for text craft, while a stale version label still required correction. In the later story proposal, useful exchanges survived review while all four illustrated boards remained marked REVISE. A recommendation from Critic is not owner approval.

Paragraph referencesE0097, E0154, E0231

Heavy work is scheduled one process at a time, with at most two active specialists. A quiet performance test cannot share its machine with a build or motion sweep and still give a useful comparison. Work has time and token limits; partial results should survive a stop. The history also records where checkpoints were missed and this discipline failed.

Paragraph referencesE0023, E0036, E0062, E0077, E0166, E0169

What this page lets you inspect

Read the chapters for the sequence, then open an event for its timestamp, participants, task and evidence status. The task inventory includes unfinished goals and completed slices. The team inventory includes temporary and archived records, so the studio does not appear to have started with its final roster. Private instructions, credentials, raw conversations and machine paths are withheld; the public references identify curated evidence.

The effort panel separates non-cached input, cached input and output. Root matched all 1,105 I1 snapshots to native numerical counters and established that mapping. Native input already includes cached input, and reasoning output is already part of output. The extension uses the same ledger fields and adjacent-delta method; actual dollar cost remains unknown.

Paragraph referencesE0370, E0414

At this cutoff the opening is dropped, unfinished and inactive. Its remaining stations, jo knowledge/hearing, all 253 offered clips across 759 detail rows and strict performance gates are preserved as unresolved. The old preview is stopped and the unstarted game-build resume is revoked. The sole objective is the paired dev-blog publication, not completion of those game gates.

Chapter 01

Before the retained log

The first retained task records are dated 02:21:38 UTC. The application log begins at 03:20:54. That gap matters: the task list tells us what was intended, but supplies no conversation to reconstruct between those times. Nine early tasks cover connection and credentials, LIMEN’s appearance, paired acting, room kits, the opening and Conveyor tutorial, independent review, a sustained Intel HD620 test and publication. They are an agenda, not nine completed results.

Paragraph referencesE0001, E0002, E0003, E0004, E0005, E0006, E0007, E0008, E0009, E0010

The studio inherited more than an agenda. Approved character and room references, existing models, lore and an opening already under development shaped the assignments. The separate owner-facing Codex chat retained runtime integration at first. Producer’s initial production work was deliberately limited to candidate deliveries that could fit that existing game. Later, Engine Master would have to preserve 260 pending relevant files as well as the recorded commit; the commit alone did not describe the project it received.

Paragraph referencesE0024, E0025, E0117, E0136

This is why the account starts before the permanent creative team. Early production belonged to Producer and the external integration context. The later roster cannot be credited retroactively with all of the assets or decisions already present. The dated tasks and handoffs give a firmer starting point than a story of a studio creating a game from nothing.

Paragraph referencesE0024, E0026, E0039, E0062

Approved references inherited by the studio: Approved art and models predate the work recorded here. Attribution and exact image versions to be supplied with the visual supplement. E0024, E0025

Chapter 02

A review build, then a failed performance test

The first two temporary intake reviewers were stopped by token caps. One used 33,944 tokens against an 18,000 cap; the next used 438,749 against 400,000. Their retained evidence was recovered rather than commissioning the intake again. The owner-facing assistant reported a budget recovery that kept the existing sandbox, circuit breakers and asset-spending cap while establishing bounded specialist work. This was an early coordination failure, not useful game progress in itself.

Paragraph referencesE0016, E0021, E0023

Production then resumed with an optimized LIMEN delivery: three detail levels retaining the rig and 20 motions. Producer prepared the offline slice; the external integrator reported it structurally verified and installed. Room work was shared in a similarly concrete way. Producer supplied briefs for five props, while the external context handled the canonical model submissions and integration, alongside the Forge and carrier. The result included an entrance, inspection desk, checkpoint portal, intake chassis and empty brain-storage crate. The early integration report recorded 710 of the project’s 1,000 model-generation credits committed; credits are not a dollar price.

Paragraph referencesE0024, E0025, E0026, E0027

By 05:04 a hosted review was reported ready. The recorded checks included 55 tests, lint, a production build and working review routes. Twelve hosted asset downloads matched their recorded hashes. Browser transport was unavailable at that point, so the build was published for review without a claim of visual, contact or performance acceptance. That historical link remains useful, but it predates the later private corrections described here.

Paragraph referencesE0027, E0028

When browser review resumed, it exposed an apparent separation between ed’s card and fingers and overlap around bob during sam’s assistance. Corrections followed. The sustained HD620 run then failed: the bridge reported 48.5 frames per second, a 33.5-millisecond 95th-percentile frame time and seven stalls longer than 100 milliseconds. Stable resources and a completed run did not clear the strict thresholds. The parent opening goal stayed open.

Paragraph referencesE0029, E0030, E0034, E0036, E0037
Evidence and handoffs · 30 events

Chronological metadata. Assignment, delivery and acceptance remain separate.

The early LIMEN and room delivery: Historical integrated candidates. A structural delivery and hosted review did not establish full-scene acceptance. E0027, E0028

Chapter 03

Specialists arrive; the handoffs need repair

A temporary character-art worker checked existing Animator evidence before the permanent Animator became active. It verified saved samples and delivery hashes while leaving visual acceptance open. Writer and Critic followed. Engine Master, 3D Artist, Scene Director, Level Designer, Visual Artist and Systems Designer joined later. The retained registry also includes an archived Writer and an earlier Scene Director record named Kelly; these are separate records, not extra simultaneous crafts.

Paragraph referencesE0043, E0047, E0050, E0064, E0066, E0067, E0069, E0092, E0100, E0105, E0114, E0115, E0123, E0124, E0127

The first LIMEN correction illustrates a failed handoff. Five sampled poses supported a candidate, but the Animator reported that the granted quiet-window confirmation had not reached its active turn. The window expired without dense validation. Producer flagged a cache dependency in the housing checker. Animator replied that its triangle records were mutable and updated in place; rebuilding the selection removed that coupling. The revised runner was not executed, and housing clearance remained unverified. The task exceeded its allowance. A later bounded run completed 3,768 of 10,000 planned records, mostly baseline coverage; all three candidate dense cases remained unstarted. An exit code of zero did not establish contact acceptance.

Paragraph referencesE0062, E0068, E0077, E0078, E0079, E0080, E0135, E0082

Writer’s first slice retained 37 of 39 spoken turns and proposed two changes, preserving bob’s approved paragraph and the current sequence. It also corrected stale continuity and transcript timing in a narrow documentation patch. Critic judged the dialogue ready for text craft but caught an old version label in the packet. The subsequent focused recheck closed that label finding. jo’s knowledge and hearing basis remained unresolved; text agreement did not settle it.

Paragraph referencesE0083, E0097, E0154

The owner then directed production through the studio. Engine Master’s canonical write attempt was denied, so the assigned work moved to a private integration candidate. It preserved the 260 pending relevant files and incorporated the reviewed dialogue and documentation changes. The boundary was recorded, not bypassed. Role memories were also corrected so earlier ownership instructions would not keep directing new work down the wrong route.

Paragraph referencesE0091, E0117, E0126, E0136, E0140, E0142, E0143
Evidence and handoffs · 104 events

Chronological metadata. Assignment, delivery and acceptance remain separate.

Chapter 04

A private build and a story beyond day one

The private cut first passed 24 focused tests, then failed to build because a dependency link pointed outside the build tool’s project root. A contained dependency copy repaired that layout. The next attempt reached compilation but was interrupted after the task exceeded its token allowance and missed its checkpoint. These were different failures, and neither was a successful build. The later studio-authored bridge execution did succeed, producing a local preview and browser captures.

Paragraph referencesE0151, E0152, E0166, E0177

Meanwhile, the owner requested a proposal for days two through fourteen. Systems Designer produced a causal draft with three plausible runs, card and relationship continuity, conditional escalation, Theatre readiness and a week-two payoff. Its first handoff was incomplete and over budget; four missing files were supplied through a bounded continuation. The saved draft gave the other crafts actual situations to work from.

Paragraph referencesE0156, E0165, E0169, E0172, E0178

Writer developed four exchanges around returning or holding a card, sharing a control under different staffing pressure, a constrained swap, and a bounded demonstration with CATHEXIS. The delivered variants tracked who could know each fact and what had actually happened before a callback. Visual Artist then produced four boards. All four needed revision: transfers, hand clearance, character identity and the visible consequences of choices were not consistently clear. Systems assembled the proposal without upgrading those boards to accepted work.

Paragraph referencesE0193, E0212, E0219

Critic recommended preserving the strongest exchanges and trying the first two encounters as a bounded prototype, subject to owner scope and taste. The combined illustrated proposal remained REVISE. Alongside it, the studio installed memory tooling and observed native discovery and indexing. That did not prove source-attributed retrieval worked in existing sessions. Recall was later saved Off while production continued; plain-file craft knowledge and individual foundation receipts remained usable.

Paragraph referencesE0231, E0183, E0184, E0185, E0186, E0187, E0202, E0207
Evidence and handoffs · 45 events

Chronological metadata. Assignment, delivery and acceptance remain separate.

Four encounter boards requiring revision: E01 card-exchange proposal: REVISE for transfer/direction and branch separation. All four encounter boards remain REVISE; this slot supplies only E01. E0212, E0231

Chapter 05

The browser finds what the tests missed

The first Director review of the private build found a speech bubble covering LIMEN’s head during the strike. Engine prepared a placement correction, and the next C1 build passed. In the later Director review, that overlap cleared in 21 inspected strike variants. This was a useful visible correction with a defined scope; the rest of the opening had not thereby passed.

Paragraph referencesE0188, E0195, E0213, E0236

Further captures found a failed image save leaving an old download link, LIMEN appearing suspended during a reading hold, and missing lines at several stops. Source review separated a genuinely silent moment from lines that should still have been visible. It also found ed’s card grip unreadable in one view. These observations became separate repair tasks rather than a blanket claim that the cut was broken in one way.

Paragraph referencesE0222, E0236

The motion inventory brought another distinction into view. All 253 selector-offered clips were retained across 759 detail rows; 37 bank entries were used by the pinned opening and 216 were offered but unused there. Those counts described membership and planned coverage, not completed motion review. Ten quiet-wait derivatives were tracked separately.

Paragraph referencesE0214

Sam’s Draw_ID and Stow_ID were authored into the sequence but absent from the metadata. A follow-up inspected all three actual assets and confirmed both motions were missing; the runtime had no fallback. A browser capture then reproduced the missing-performance error and blocked continuation. A metadata change could not supply the absent movement. The next task therefore had to deliver real transitions while preserving the card sequence and unrelated motions.

Paragraph referencesE0220, E0224, E0233, E0240

Two identified browser exports. This is scoped placement evidence, not a claim of pixel-identical framing or accepted grounding/contact.

Evidence and handoffs · 44 events

Chronological metadata. Assignment, delivery and acceptance remain separate.

Strike speech before and after placement correction: Producer-inspected exports at 14.65 seconds show the bubble moved right and LIMEN’s head exposed. Director cleared the sampled overlay defect; grounding and contact remain separate open questions. E0188, E0236

Chapter 06

Two presentation fixes; an unresolved body fit

The missing speech received a focused trace. At three ordinary reading stops, the full lines existed but their offscreen anchors were hidden. Engine prepared a named offscreen reading card beside Continue and a matching exported line, preserving the words and timing. Separately, the export repair cleared stale results and prevented late or failed saves from publishing an old image. Focused checks passed; rendered acceptance of the combined layers still required a new build.

Paragraph referencesE0249, E0257, E0267

Grounding also required a narrower diagnosis than simply moving LIMEN down. Measurements varied across held poses, with a separate body-to-plane gap at the strike. A uniform lowering would not follow from that evidence. The investigation produced a measured distinction and a further review requirement, not an accepted posture correction.

Paragraph referencesE0263, E0264, E0271

Sam’s first new transition candidate completed processing but failed contact checks: self intersections and card-to-body intersections remained across the three detail levels. The next repair also failed. Investigation localized a collision at the transfer pose itself, before the path between poses. Preserving a defective endpoint could not make the action work. The waist route was rejected, and a possible holder became the next proposed implementation.

Paragraph referencesE0252, E0268, E0269, E0277, E0279, E0280

The presentation work was allowed to proceed separately, with failed Sam motions excluded. That separation protected a usable correction from an unresolved animation problem. It also left a hard obligation: supplying source files or diagnostics for sam was not equivalent to delivering a watchable retrieval, presentation and stow.

Paragraph referencesE0270, E0276, E0282, E0365
Evidence and handoffs · 50 events

Chronological metadata. Assignment, delivery and acceptance remain separate.

Chapter 07

The holder detour and the build detour

The first holder was delivered as an unplaced prototype. Its endpoint-fit run stopped before fitting because the actual shell disagreed with the analytic representation. After that representation was corrected, six real endpoint cases ran and all failed. This second result established a physical fit failure; the first had established a precondition defect. Neither justified exporting a least-bad motion.

Paragraph referencesE0295, E0325, E0327, E0329

A revised holder changed the opening and mounting interface, but old poses still intersected it and no endpoint was accepted. The work had produced more exact hardware and diagnostic evidence while leaving the required action unseen. Native Meshy access checks also exposed a separate tool boundary: the crafts could not write the canonical accounting lock. Those checks and the work to establish a supported route belong in the record, but are not holder animation results.

Paragraph referencesE0294, E0296, E0297, E0307, E0308, E0310, E0317, E0342

The presentation build met a resource barrier in parallel. Available memory was below its original admission requirement. Measurements showed that parking five idle role processes would not demonstrably recover enough, so that advice was not presented as a solution. A small containment check preceded a separately authorized, resource-limited build experiment.

Paragraph referencesE0288, E0301, E0306, E0316, E0322, E0324

The first contained attempt failed before compilation because the execution handoff omitted a process-group field required by reused validation. Later checks caught another mismatch: a legitimate change in the old server’s parent process would be rejected. Engine corrected the handoff and tested the actual validation chain. These were concrete wrapper defects, not evidence that the compiler needed more memory or that the game had built successfully.

Paragraph referencesE0331, E0332, E0349, E0350, E0356
Evidence and handoffs · 56 events

Chronological metadata. Assignment, delivery and acceptance remain separate.

Chapter 08

A preview, a lint stop and the owner’s intervention

A bounded browser pass loaded all ten workers’ default floor previews and checked navigation and Play/Pause. Director then found max’s arms-out pose unsuitable as a representative relaxed default. Still images of sam and ben obscured faces or torsos, but could not establish what their motion meant; kit’s pipe was unreadable in its view, not proven absent. Six other samples had no specific still-image defect. The preview used Idle, while ordinary Security waiting used Quiet_Wait with exceptions. Those are different selections, and the full motion bank remained unreviewed.

Paragraph referencesE0347, E0359

The next Sam surface diagnostic rejected two proposed mounting surfaces before evaluating any contact cases. It was not another six-pose fit and not a contact pass. Meanwhile, the corrected contained build applied both presentation patches and stopped at one prefer-const lint error before compilation began. The old C1 server had been stopped as designed. The saved build output remained, but there was no replacement preview running from the corrected source at this cutoff.

Paragraph referencesE0357, E0360, E0362

At 21:16:53, the owner challenged the time and tokens spent on the holder. The pending H2 diagnostic was withdrawn before launch, further holder work stopped, and the build correction continued. There was still no accepted holder or sam draw/stow performance. Producer’s reassessment acknowledged that isolated hardware and diagnostic deliveries had substituted for a reviewable action. Its proposed next route starts with reachable, readable character poses and the complete visible performance; it is a recommendation, not an execution grant or a solved fit.

Paragraph referencesE0365, E0366, E0367

The original 21:20 cutoff closes with useful work that can be inspected: optimized and integrated early assets, a historical hosted review, successful private cuts, narrowly reviewed dialogue, an illustrated story proposal with explicit revisions, and specific browser defects traced to causes. It also closes with failed performance acceptance, unresolved acting and full-scene review, and a build correction still in progress. The retained history should let the next decision start from those actual results. The following chapter records the owner’s replacement of that objective.

Paragraph referencesE0028, E0037, E0097, E0177, E0213, E0231, E0236, E0257, E0359, E0360, E0365

Chapter 09

The owner replaces the goal; nine crafts reflect

At 21:23 the owner replaced the unfinished opening goal with a dev blog. The request reached Producer’s active turn at 21:44. The ready but unstarted game-build resume was revoked. Existing source, failed candidates and unresolved scene/performance findings were preserved. No later publication of this account is an opening acceptance.

Paragraph referencesE0370, E0385, E0386, E0398, E0400

Writer completed the supporting history, including a correction that separated Producer’s cache allegation from Animator’s reply about mutable geometry. Critic independently flagged the earlier overstatement and the ambiguity of reported input. Producer retained the corrected history and terminology change. Engine delivered a static history-page candidate with focused source checks; no rendered page or publication was claimed.

Paragraph referencesE0377, E0378, E0379, E0385, E0401, E0404

Producer authored five shared questions and one craft-specific question, then interviewed all nine current specialists with at most two assignments active. All nine returned six actual answers. Level Designer explicitly reported receiving no production task. The complete answers and exact selected quotations accompany Producer’s independently authored first post; Writer did not ghostwrite it.

Paragraph referencesE0389, E0390, E0391, E0392, E0393, E0394, E0395, E0396, E0397, E0402, E0405, E0406, E0409, E0410, E0415, E0416, E0419, E0420

The owner required exact model and usage reporting and a separate outside systems assessment after Producer’s reflection was finished. Root supplied factual runtime metadata and numerical token-field reconciliation without a reflection thesis. The native Critic is an interviewee and page reviewer, not the external assessor. At this cutoff the external assessment had not been commissioned, and neither report had been published. Publication must pair the two separately attributed reports.

Paragraph referencesE0375, E0411, E0414

Chronological metadata. Assignment, delivery and acceptance remain separate.

What the effort figures can tell us

At the owner’s intervention, holder work had spanned 2 hours 16 minutes 48 seconds; the missing-cue investigation had spanned 4 hours 14 minutes 33 seconds. These are overlapping elapsed windows, not summed active labour. Two scoped counter differences establish a conservative lower bound of 9,618,332 tokens, including cached input. It excludes initial holder work, earlier repair and Producer/bridge coordination. A broader 18,106,021-token window mixes in access checks and is not an exact holder cost.

The native metadata packet covers 15 sessions; it is not the same coverage or method as these ledger deltas. The separate statistics appendix shows every session and the earlier I1 comparison.

Source coverage and gaps

Public history contains curated metadata, not raw private messages or source directories.

  • Tasks predate retained event log; no invented intervening conversation.
  • Only retained closed mail is read. Live inboxes excluded except the explicitly provided owner request; later filing may expose more historical bodies.
  • External bridge work is represented by reports/artifacts, not its full native transcript or tokens.
  • Task status snapshots were extracted after cutoff; dated source claims take precedence.
  • Agent session counters cannot be precisely allocated to tasks; initial snapshots and resets leave gaps.
  • Sources establish reports and artifacts, not unseen visual acceptance.
  • Pre-studio approved art, models and canonical work must be credited as inherited.
  • Public source catalog deliberately omits original paths, session IDs, message bodies, terminal dumps and sensitive setup screenshots.
